2. PROJECT IDENTITY
Project

COREI — Institutional Operating Environment

COREI is being developed as an institutional-grade operating environment for professional trading, financial intelligence, infrastructure operations, decision support, portfolio operations, execution, risk, research, automation, AI, security, observability, and institutional governance.

The platform is intended to support:

multi-account operations;
multi-broker operations;
multi-strategy operations;
multi-portfolio operations;
multi-asset operations;
multi-market operations;
multi-exchange operations;
multi-region operations;
multi-currency operations;
multi-custodian operations;
multi-jurisdiction operations;
multi-team operations;
multi-role operations;
multi-monitor workstations;
24×7 operations;
institutional governance;
high-scale decision making;
professional operational workflows.

COREI is not intended to become merely:

a dashboard;
a trading terminal;
a charting application;
a Kubernetes frontend;
a collection of widgets;
a VS Code clone;
a Grafana clone;
a TradingView clone.

It is an institutional operating environment that integrates and operationalizes specialized professional systems behind COREI-owned architecture boundaries.

11. CRITICAL TECHNOLOGY AND TOOLCHAIN GOVERNANCE

The newly reviewed authoritative DOC-03 — COREI Technology Architecture must be treated as a Technology Constitution, not merely an inventory.

Its explicit principle is that every technology must have:

architectural purpose;
institutional justification;
defined ownership;
lifecycle state;
integration boundary;
operational responsibility.

Technology must solve institutional problems rather than be adopted merely because it is popular.

Permanent rule

COREI already possesses and/or has adopted a substantial professional technology ecosystem.

These technologies must be considered during every applicable future phase.

The engineering decision must be:

DOES AN APPROVED / PRODUCTION / FROZEN PROFESSIONAL TOOL
ALREADY SOLVE THIS PROBLEM?

If yes:

INTEGRATE IT
ADAPT IT
WRAP IT
ORCHESTRATE IT
EXPOSE IT THROUGH COREI

Do not:

REBUILD IT FROM SCRATCH

unless an explicit architecture decision establishes that:

the existing technology cannot satisfy the requirement;
integration would violate COREI architecture;
replacement is institutionally justified;
replacement cost has been evaluated;
the change is formally governed.

DOC-03 explicitly establishes technology lifecycle, classification, ownership, decision history, replacement complexity, and institutional evaluation as governing concerns.

runtime;
state;

capability semantics;
institutional behavior.

docking mechanics.

This same principle applies throughout the platform.

17. IMPORTANT TOOLCHAIN DECISION RULE

The existence of a tool does not automatically mean it must be integrated immediately.

The rule is:

RIGHT TOOL
+
RIGHT ARCHITECTURAL OWNER
+
RIGHT PHASE
+
RIGHT STEP
+
RIGHT INTEGRATION BOUNDARY
=
AUTHORIZED INTEGRATION

Therefore, avoid both extremes:

Wrong approach A
"We installed the tool, therefore integrate it now."
Wrong approach B
"We can code this ourselves, therefore ignore the installed professional tool."
Correct approach
Identify requirement
        ↓
Check phase ownership
        ↓
Check existing COREI capability
        ↓
Check approved professional technology
        ↓
Determine integration boundary
        ↓
Integrate at the authorized phase/step
        ↓
Validate
        ↓
Document ownership

STEP-03 — PROVIDER BINDING MODEL
Objective

capability_id

priority
scope
configuration_reference
activation_policy
availability_policy
fallback_policy
binding_state

explicit;
deterministic;
inspectable;
replaceable;
version-aware;
scope-aware;
lifecycle-aware;
auditable.

    ↓
Provider Contract
    ↓
Provider Implementation

runtime state.

+

hidden fallback;

bypassing registries.

not:

adapter failure
dependency failure
configuration failure
resolution failure

deterministic;
observable;
diagnosable;
non-silent;
propagated through approved platform mechanisms.
Fallback Rule

NO:
Provider A failed
→ silently use Provider B

YES:
Provider A failed
→ evaluate explicit approved fallback policy
→ deterministically resolve allowed Provider B
→ record transition

const fs = require("fs");

const pkg = JSON.parse(
    fs.readFileSync("package.json", "utf8")
);

const sections = [
    "dependencies",
    "devDependencies",
    "peerDependencies",
    "optionalDependencies"
];

for (const section of sections) {
    console.log(`\n[${section}]`);

    const entries = Object.entries(
        pkg[section] ?? {}
    ).sort(([a], [b]) => a.localeCompare(b));

    if (entries.length === 0) {
        console.log("(none)");
        continue;
    }

}

2. Step-02 Provider Taxonomy

Every discovered provider must be classified into one of these categories:

A — CAPABILITY_PROVIDER
    Supplies an implementation of a COREI capability contract.

B — TECHNOLOGY_ADAPTER
    Translates an external/professional technology into a COREI contract.

C — CONTEXT_PROVIDER
    React/context composition and dependency delivery.

D — PLATFORM_PROVIDER
    Provides a broader platform-level composition boundary.

E — DOMAIN_PROVIDER
    Provides a domain-specific runtime/context boundary.

F — SERVICE_PROVIDER
    Supplies or exposes a service integration.

G — LEGACY_OR_TRANSITIONAL
    Historical or migration surface requiring preservation analysis.

H — UNRESOLVED
    Ownership cannot yet be proven from repository evidence.

This taxonomy is a Step-02 analysis classification, not authorization to rename existing files.

#!/usr/bin/env bash

set -uo pipefail

WORKSPACE_ROOT="$HOME/corei/ui/corei-os"
OUTPUT_ROOT="/tmp/corei-phase18-step02"

cd "$WORKSPACE_ROOT" || exit 1

rm -rf "$OUTPUT_ROOT"
mkdir -p "$OUTPUT_ROOT"

REPORT="$OUTPUT_ROOT/phase18-step02-contract-mapping.txt"

exec > >(tee "$REPORT") 2>&1

section() {
    echo
    echo "================================================================================"
    echo "$1"
    echo "================================================================================"
}

print_file() {
    local file="$1"

    if [ -f "$file" ]; then
        echo
        echo "--------------------------------------------------------------------------------"
        echo "FILE: $file"
        echo "--------------------------------------------------------------------------------"
        sed -n '1,320p' "$file"
    fi
}

section "COREI — STAGE-25 PHASE-18 STEP-02"
echo "CAPABILITY ↔ PROVIDER CONTRACT MAPPING"
echo
echo "MODE: READ-ONLY REPOSITORY RECONCILIATION"

echo "DEPENDENCY INSTALLATION: FORBIDDEN"
echo "BINDING ENGINE IMPLEMENTATION: FORBIDDEN"
echo "RESOLUTION ENGINE IMPLEMENTATION: FORBIDDEN"
echo "PHASE-19 WORK: FORBIDDEN"

section "01 — REPOSITORY IDENTITY"

printf 'CURRENT_BRANCH: '
git branch --show-current 2>/dev/null || true

printf 'HEAD: '
git rev-parse HEAD 2>/dev/null || true

printf 'TREE: '
git rev-parse HEAD^{tree} 2>/dev/null || true

printf 'PHASE-17_FREEZE_TAG_TARGET: '
git rev-list -n 1 stage25-phase17-institutional-shell-v1 2>/dev/null || true

section "02 — PRE-STEP GIT STATE"

git status --short > "$OUTPUT_ROOT/git-status-before.txt"
cat "$OUTPUT_ROOT/git-status-before.txt"

section "03 — CAPABILITY PLATFORM FILE INVENTORY"

if [ -d src/capabilities ]; then
    find src/capabilities -type f | sort \
        > "$OUTPUT_ROOT/capability-files.txt"

else
    echo "src/capabilities — ABSENT"
fi

section "04 — CAPABILITY DIRECTORY MODEL"

if [ -d src/capabilities ]; then
    find src/capabilities \
        -type d \
        | sort
fi

section "05 — CAPABILITY PUBLIC API SURFACES"

if [ -d src/capabilities ]; then
    while IFS= read -r file; do
        print_file "$file"
    done < <(
        find src/capabilities \
            -type f \
            \( -name 'index.ts' -o -name 'index.tsx' \) \
            | sort
    )
fi

section "06 — CAPABILITY CONTRACT FILES"

find src/capabilities \
    -type f \
    \( \
        -iname '*contract*.ts' \
        -o \
        -iname '*contract*.tsx' \
        -o \
        -iname '*types*.ts' \
        -o \
        -iname '*model*.ts' \
    \) \
    2>/dev/null \
    | sort \
    > "$OUTPUT_ROOT/capability-contract-files.txt"

cat "$OUTPUT_ROOT/capability-contract-files.txt"

while IFS= read -r file; do
    print_file "$file"
done < "$OUTPUT_ROOT/capability-contract-files.txt"

section "07 — CAPABILITY DEFINITIONS"

grep -RInE \

1. Step-03 Objective

Establish a deterministic, vendor-independent relationship:

CAPABILITY ID
        │
        ▼

2. Architectural Boundary

The authoritative separation is:

CAPABILITY PLATFORM
    owns:
    - capability identity
    - capability contracts
    - capability requirements
    - capability state
    - capability dependencies

PROVIDER PLATFORM
    owns:
    - provider identity
    - provider contracts
    - provider metadata
    - provider registry
    - provider lifecycle

PHASE-18 INTEGRATION LAYER
    owns:
    - capability ↔ provider relationship
    - binding metadata
    - binding registry
    - binding lookup

FUTURE PHASE-18 STEPS
    own:
    - validation
    - resolution
    - activation
    - runtime orchestration

The integration layer must reference the two existing platforms.

It must not absorb or duplicate them.

3. Binding Cardinality

Step-03 must not assume:

1 capability = 1 provider

That would be too restrictive for the institutional architecture.

The binding model must permit:

CAPABILITY A
    ├── PROVIDER 1
    ├── PROVIDER 2
    └── PROVIDER 3

because COREI may eventually support:

editor capability
    ├── monaco
    └── another approved editor provider

chart capability
    ├── echarts
    └── another approved chart provider

grid capability
    ├── ag-grid
    └── another approved grid provider

But Step-03 also requires deterministic preference.

Therefore each binding records:

capabilityId
providerId
priority
enabled

This provides:

ONE CAPABILITY
        ↓
ZERO OR MORE PROVIDERS
        ↓
DETERMINISTIC ORDER

/**
 * =============================================================================
 * COREI OPERATING SYSTEM
 *
 * Stage       : 25
 * Phase       : 18
 * Step        : 03
 *
 * File        : capability-provider-binding.ts
 * Purpose     : Capability ↔ Provider Binding Contract
 *
 * -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
 * RESPONSIBILITY
 * -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
 *
 * Defines the deterministic relationship between an existing COREI capability
 * identity and an existing COREI provider identity.
 *
 * This contract represents integration metadata only.
 *
 * It does not:
 *
 * - own capability definitions,
 * - own provider definitions,
 * - resolve providers,
 * - initialize providers,
 * - dispose providers,
 * - load technology implementations,
 * - execute capability runtime behavior.
 *
 * =============================================================================
 */

export interface CapabilityProviderBinding {

    /**
     * Existing capability identity.
     */
    readonly capabilityId: string;

    /**
     * Existing provider identity.
     */
    readonly providerId: string;

    /**
     * Deterministic provider preference.
     *
     * Lower numeric values represent higher preference.
     */
    readonly priority: number;

    /**
     * Controls whether the binding participates in future
     * validation and resolution.
     */
    readonly enabled: boolean;

}
TS

/**
 * =============================================================================
 * COREI OPERATING SYSTEM
 *
 * Stage       : 25
 * Phase       : 18
 * Step        : 03
 *

 *

 * relationships across the Capability Provider Integration boundary.
 *

 */

} from "./capability-provider-binding";

TS

echo

cat > "$STEP_ROOT/capability-provider-binding-registry.ts" <<'TS'
/**
 * =============================================================================
 * COREI OPERATING SYSTEM
 *
 * Stage       : 25
 * Phase       : 18
 * Step        : 03
 *
 * File        : capability-provider-binding-registry.ts
 * Purpose     : Capability ↔ Provider Binding Registry
 *
 * -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
 * RESPONSIBILITY
 * -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
 *
 * Owns the in-memory registration and deterministic lookup of relationships
 * between existing capability identities and existing provider identities.
 *
 * The registry stores relationship metadata only.
 *
 * It does not:
 *
 * - validate capability existence,
 * - validate provider existence,
 * - resolve provider implementations,
 * - initialize providers,
 * - dispose providers,
 * - execute provider lifecycle behavior.
 *
 * -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
 * DETERMINISM
 * -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
 *
 * Capability lookups are ordered by:
 *
 * 1. ascending priority,
 * 2. provider identity as a stable lexical tie-breaker.
 *
 * =============================================================================
 */

import type {

    CapabilityProviderBinding

} from "./capability-provider-binding";

import type {

    CapabilityProviderBindings

} from "./capability-provider-bindings";

export class CapabilityProviderBindingRegistry {

    private readonly bindings:
        CapabilityProviderBinding[] = [];

    /**
     * Registers a capability-to-provider relationship.
     *
     * An identical capability/provider pair is stored only once.
     */
    public register(
        binding: CapabilityProviderBinding
    ): void {

        const existingBinding =
            this.find(
                binding.capabilityId,
                binding.providerId
            );

        if (
            existingBinding
        ) {

            return;

        }

        this.bindings.push(
            binding
        );

    }

    /**
     * Returns every registered binding.
     */
    public getAll():
        CapabilityProviderBindings {

        return [
            ...this.bindings
        ];

    }

    /**
     * Returns bindings associated with a capability identity
     * in deterministic preference order.
     */
    public findByCapabilityId(
        capabilityId: string
    ): CapabilityProviderBindings {

        return this.bindings
            .filter(
                binding =>
                    binding.capabilityId ===
                    capabilityId
            )
            .sort(
                (
                    left,
                    right
                ) => {

                    const priorityDifference =
                        left.priority -
                        right.priority;

                    if (
                        priorityDifference !== 0
                    ) {

                        return priorityDifference;

                    }

                    return left.providerId.localeCompare(
                        right.providerId
                    );

                }
            );

    }

    /**
     * Returns bindings associated with a provider identity.
     */
    public findByProviderId(
        providerId: string
    ): CapabilityProviderBindings {

        return this.bindings.filter(
            binding =>
                binding.providerId ===
                providerId
        );

    }

    /**
     * Returns the exact capability/provider relationship,
     * when registered.
     */
    public find(
        capabilityId: string,
        providerId: string
    ): CapabilityProviderBinding | undefined {

        return this.bindings.find(
            binding =>
                binding.capabilityId ===
                    capabilityId &&
                binding.providerId ===
                    providerId
        );

    }

}
TS

echo
echo "================================================================================"
echo "06 — CREATE INTEGRATION PUBLIC API"
echo "================================================================================"

The Step-03 terminal evidence confirms the binding contract, registry, TypeScript validation, production build, and circular-dependency validation all passed.

1. Step-04 Objective

Implement a pure deterministic provider-resolution boundary that consumes the certified Step-03 binding registry.

Target flow:

capabilityId
    │
    ▼
CapabilityProviderBindingRegistry
    │
    ▼
All bindings for capability
    │
    ▼
Enabled bindings only
    │
    ▼
Already deterministic order
    │
    ▼
First eligible binding
    │
    ▼
Resolved provider identity

The resolution engine will return resolution metadata, not a provider instance.

2. Critical Architecture Boundary

Step-04 owns:

REQUEST
    ↓
LOOKUP
    ↓
FILTER
    ↓
SELECT
    ↓
RETURN RESOLUTION RESULT

Step-04 does not own:

Provider initialization
Provider activation
Provider disposal
Provider instance construction
Technology adapter execution
Health checking
Failover orchestration
Dynamic imports
Dependency installation
React context
Workspace composition
Phase-19 work

The separation remains:

STEP-03
BINDING
    ↓
STEP-04
RESOLUTION
    ↓
STEP-05
LIFECYCLE INTEGRATION
    ↓
STEP-06
RUNTIME INTEGRATION
3. Resolution Semantics

For a capability:

capability.editor

suppose bindings are:

provider-beta   priority=10 enabled=true
provider-alpha  priority=10 enabled=true
provider-gamma  priority=20 enabled=true
provider-old    priority=1  enabled=false

Step-03 produces deterministic ordering:

provider-old
provider-alpha
provider-beta
provider-gamma

Step-04 filters disabled bindings:

provider-alpha
provider-beta
provider-gamma

and resolves:

provider-alpha

Therefore:

DISABLED BINDING
    ≠
ELIGIBLE BINDING

and:

NO ELIGIBLE BINDING
    ≠
EXCEPTION

At this stage, absence is represented explicitly as a resolution outcome.

4. Resolution Result Model

Step-04 will use an explicit discriminated result:

RESOLVED

or:

UNRESOLVED

This is preferable to returning only:

string | undefined

because future lifecycle/runtime integration requires a stable semantic boundary.

The result remains intentionally small:

CapabilityProviderResolution
├── status
├── capabilityId
├── providerId       when RESOLVED
└── binding          when RESOLVED

No failure taxonomy, health state, fallback history, runtime instance, or lifecycle state is introduced yet.

Create
src/capabilities/integration/
├── capability-provider-resolution.ts
└── capability-provider-resolver.ts
Update
src/capabilities/integration/index.ts

That is the complete authorized Step-04 source scope.

Do not modify
src/providers/**
src/foundation/capabilities/**
src/foundation/workspace/**
src/shell/**
src/workbench/**
src/app/**
package.json
pnpm-lock.yaml

Step-04 also does not need to modify:

because Step-03 already exports the entire integration public API.

/**
 * =============================================================================
 * COREI OPERATING SYSTEM
 *
 * Stage       : 25
 * Phase       : 18
 * Step        : 04
 *
 * File        : capability-provider-resolution.ts
 * Purpose     : Capability Provider Resolution Result Model
 *
 * -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
 * RESPONSIBILITY
 * -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
 *
 * Defines the explicit outcome of deterministic capability-to-provider
 * resolution.
 *
 * Resolution returns provider identity and binding metadata only.
 *
 * It does not:
 *
 * - instantiate a provider,
 * - initialize a provider,
 * - activate a provider,
 * - dispose a provider,
 * - execute a technology adapter,
 * - evaluate provider health,
 * - perform runtime failover.
 *
 * =============================================================================
 */

import type {

    CapabilityProviderBinding

} from "./capability-provider-binding";

/**
 * Successful deterministic provider resolution.
 */
export interface ResolvedCapabilityProvider {

    readonly status: "resolved";

    readonly capabilityId: string;

    readonly providerId: string;

    readonly binding: CapabilityProviderBinding;

}

/**
 * Explicit absence of an eligible provider binding.
 */
export interface UnresolvedCapabilityProvider {

    readonly status: "unresolved";

    readonly capabilityId: string;

}

/**
 * Complete provider-resolution result.
 */
export type CapabilityProviderResolution =

    | ResolvedCapabilityProvider
    | UnresolvedCapabilityProvider;
TS

echo
echo "================================================================================"
echo "06 — CREATE PROVIDER RESOLVER"
echo "================================================================================"

cat > "$INTEGRATION_ROOT/capability-provider-resolver.ts" <<'TS'
/**
 * =============================================================================
 * COREI OPERATING SYSTEM
 *
 * Stage       : 25
 * Phase       : 18
 * Step        : 04
 *
 * File        : capability-provider-resolver.ts
 * Purpose     : Deterministic Capability Provider Resolver
 *
 * -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
 * RESPONSIBILITY
 * -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
 *
 * Resolves the preferred enabled provider binding for an existing capability
 * identity.
 *
 * Resolution consumes the deterministic ordering owned by the
 * CapabilityProviderBindingRegistry.
 *
 * The resolver:
 *
 * 1. requests bindings for a capability identity,
 * 2. excludes disabled bindings,
 * 3. selects the first remaining deterministic binding,
 * 4. returns an explicit resolved or unresolved result.
 *
 * -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
 * BOUNDARY
 * -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
 *
 * This resolver returns provider identity and binding metadata only.
 *
 * It does not:
 *
 * - validate provider runtime availability,
 * - initialize providers,
 * - activate providers,
 * - dispose providers,
 * - instantiate technologies,
 * - execute adapters,
 * - perform health-aware failover.
 *
 * =============================================================================
 */

import type {

    CapabilityProviderBindingRegistry

} from "./capability-provider-binding-registry";

import type {

    CapabilityProviderResolution

} from "./capability-provider-resolution";

export class CapabilityProviderResolver {

    public constructor(

        private readonly bindingRegistry:
            CapabilityProviderBindingRegistry

    ) {}

    /**
     * Resolves the highest-preference enabled provider binding
     * for the requested capability identity.
     */
    public resolve(
        capabilityId: string
    ): CapabilityProviderResolution {

        const eligibleBinding =
            this.bindingRegistry
                .findByCapabilityId(
                    capabilityId
                )
                .find(
                    binding =>
                        binding.enabled
                );

        if (
            !eligibleBinding
        ) {

            return {

                status: "unresolved",
                capabilityId

            };

        }

        return {

            status: "resolved",
            capabilityId,
            providerId:
                eligibleBinding.providerId,
            binding:
                eligibleBinding

        };

    }

}
TS

if not content.endswith("\n"):

for export_line in required_exports:
    if export_line not in content:
        content += f"{export_line}\n"

path.write_text(content)

import {

    CapabilityProviderBindingRegistry,
    CapabilityProviderResolver

} from "../../home/coreeye/corei/ui/corei-os/src/capabilities/integration";

const registry =
    new CapabilityProviderBindingRegistry();

registry.register({

    capabilityId: "capability.test",
    providerId: "provider.gamma",
    priority: 20,
    enabled: true

});

registry.register({

    capabilityId: "capability.test",
    providerId: "provider.beta",
    priority: 10,
    enabled: true

});

registry.register({

    capabilityId: "capability.test",
    providerId: "provider.alpha",
    priority: 10,
    enabled: true

});

registry.register({

    capabilityId: "capability.test",
    providerId: "provider.disabled",
    priority: 1,
    enabled: false

});

const resolver =
    new CapabilityProviderResolver(
        registry
    );

    resolver.resolve(
        "capability.test"
    );

    resolved.status !== "resolved"
) {

    throw new Error(
        "Expected capability.test to resolve."
    );

    resolved.providerId !==
    "provider.alpha"
) {

        `Expected provider.alpha, received ${resolved.providerId}.`

}

const unresolved =
    resolver.resolve(
        "capability.missing"
    );

if (
    unresolved.status !==
    "unresolved"
) {

    throw new Error(
        "Expected capability.missing to remain unresolved."
    );

}

console.log(
    "DETERMINISTIC_RESOLUTION_BEHAVIOR=PASS"
);

from pathlib import Path

registry_path = Path(
    "src/capabilities/integration/"
    "capability-provider-binding-registry.ts"
)

resolver_path = Path(
    "src/capabilities/integration/"
    "capability-provider-resolver.ts"
)

registry = registry_path.read_text()
resolver = resolver_path.read_text()

required_registry_semantics = [
    "left.priority -",
    "right.priority",
    "left.providerId.localeCompare(",
    "right.providerId",
]

required_resolver_semantics = [
    ".findByCapabilityId(",
    ".find(",
    "binding.enabled",
    'status: "unresolved"',
    'status: "resolved"',
    "eligibleBinding.providerId",
]

missing = []

for token in required_registry_semantics:
    if token not in registry:
        missing.append(
            f"registry:{token}"
        )

for token in required_resolver_semantics:
    if token not in resolver:
        missing.append(
            f"resolver:{token}"
        )

if missing:
    raise SystemExit(
        "STATIC_DETERMINISM_CHECK=FAIL\n"
        + "\n".join(missing)
    )

print(
    "STATIC_DETERMINISM_CHECK=PASS"
)
PY

Establish a deterministic integration boundary that can:

accept a successful capability-provider resolution;

determine whether that provider is lifecycle-eligible for later runtime consumption;
return an explicit integration result.

3. Mandatory Pre-Implementation Reconciliation

Step-02 proved that the Provider Platform already contains its own:

contracts;
models;
registry;
manager;
runtime;
lifecycle.

Therefore we must not guess the exact provider registry and lifecycle APIs.

Before creating Step-05 integration code, the implementation script will first inspect the existing Provider Platform and deterministically identify the actual public API.

This is important because Step-05 must consume the existing architecture rather than invent assumptions such as:

providerRegistry.get()
provider.state
provider.status
provider.lifecycleState

without repository evidence.

Accordingly, Step-05 is executed in two controlled passes:

PASS A — LIFECYCLE CONTRACT RECONCILIATION
PASS B — LIFECYCLE INTEGRATION IMPLEMENTATION

This avoids architecture drift.

#!/usr/bin/env bash

set -euo pipefail

WORKSPACE_ROOT="$HOME/corei/ui/corei-os"

cd "$WORKSPACE_ROOT"

mkdir -p "$EVIDENCE_ROOT"

exec > >(tee "$REPORT") 2>&1

section() {
    echo
    echo "================================================================================"
    echo "$1"
    echo "================================================================================"
}

print_file() {
    local file="$1"

    if [ -f "$file" ]; then
        echo
        echo "--------------------------------------------------------------------------------"
        echo "FILE: $file"
        echo "--------------------------------------------------------------------------------"
        sed -n '1,360p' "$file"
    fi
}

section "COREI — STAGE-25 PHASE-18 STEP-05"

5. Why Step-05 Is Split Into Two Passes

This is deliberate.

The existing Provider Platform is frozen architecture that Phase-18 must consume. We must first determine the exact existing lifecycle API before writing integration code.

The wrong approach would be:

ASSUME PROVIDER API
      ↓
WRITE INTEGRATION
      ↓
DISCOVER CONTRACT MISMATCH
      ↓
PATCH EXISTING PROVIDER PLATFORM

The correct institutional process is:

DESCRIBE
      ↓
DISCOVER EXISTING CONTRACT
      ↓
RECONCILE OWNERSHIP
      ↓
IMPLEMENT AGAINST REAL API
      ↓
VALIDATE
      ↓
CERTIFY

Provider identity
What is the authoritative provider ID field?
Provider lookup
Which existing registry/manager owns provider lookup?
Provider lifecycle
What exact lifecycle states already exist?
Provider lifecycle authority
Does lifecycle state live in:
- provider model,
- registry,
- manager,
- runtime,
- dedicated lifecycle controller?
Eligibility semantics
Which existing state means:
- available for lifecycle integration,
- not yet ready,
- active,
- inactive,
- failed,
- unavailable?
Integration import boundary
What is the correct public API import path?

Only after those facts are known will Pass B implement the lifecycle integration.

8. Step-05 Lifecycle Result Principle

The integration should eventually distinguish explicit outcomes equivalent to:

CAPABILITY UNRESOLVED
PROVIDER NOT FOUND
PROVIDER LIFECYCLE INELIGIBLE
PROVIDER LIFECYCLE ELIGIBLE

However, the exact result vocabulary must reflect the actual Provider Platform lifecycle semantics.

We will not invent a second state vocabulary such as:

REGISTERED
INITIALIZING
READY
ACTIVE
DEGRADED
FAILED

unless those states already exist or are explicitly required by the existing authoritative contracts.

1. Authoritative Lifecycle Reconciliation Result

The most important finding is that the Provider Platform does not currently have one universal lifecycle implementation.

Instead, it has two distinct levels.

Generic Provider Platform
ProviderContract
    ↓
ProviderModel
    ↓
ProviderRegistry
    ↓
ProviderManager
    ↓
ProviderRuntime

Its runtime state is only:

initialized: boolean
loaded: boolean

The generic registry is currently:

ProviderRegistry = []

and the generic manager exposes only:

getAll()

There is no generic:

findById()
initialize()
activate()
deactivate()
dispose()

lifecycle API.

UI Provider Platform

The concrete provider population currently lives here:

UIProviderContract
    ↓
UIProviderModel
    ↓
UIProviderRegistry
    ↓
UIProviderManager
    ↓
UIProviderRuntime
    ↓
UIProviderLifecycle

The UI registry contains:

MonacoProvider
DockviewProvider
XtermProvider
AGGridProvider
EChartsProvider
ReactFlowProvider

The authoritative lookup API is:

UIProviderManager.findById(id)

The authoritative lifecycle vocabulary is:

REGISTERED
INITIALIZED
ACTIVE
INACTIVE

with:

state
initialized
activated

The current default lifecycle is:

state       = REGISTERED
initialized = false
activated   = false

2. Critical Architectural Finding

Step-05 must not create a second lifecycle state machine.

The existing Provider Platform already owns:

UIProviderState
UIProviderLifecycle
DefaultUIProviderLifecycle
UIProviderRuntime

Therefore the Phase-18 integration layer must consume those existing contracts.

The correct architecture is:

CapabilityProviderResolution
        │
        ├── unresolved
        │      ↓
        │   lifecycle integration:
        │   capability_unresolved
        │
        └── resolved
               │
               ▼
           providerId
               │
               ▼
       UIProviderManager.findById()
               │
        ┌──────┴──────┐
        │             │
     missing        found
        │             │
        ▼             ▼
 provider_not_found   existing
                      UIProviderModel
                          │
                          ▼
                  UIProviderLifecycle
                          │
                          ▼
                  eligibility result
3. Important Limitation Discovered

There is currently no per-provider lifecycle state storage.

The evidence shows:

UIProviderRuntime
    └── lifecycle:
        typeof DefaultUIProviderLifecycle

and:

DefaultUIProviderLifecycle
    state: REGISTERED
    initialized: false
    activated: false

This means the existing architecture currently exposes a default lifecycle contract/state, not a mutable provider-specific lifecycle registry keyed by providerId.

Therefore Step-05 must not falsely implement:

provider-A → ACTIVE
provider-B → INITIALIZED
provider-C → INACTIVE

because the existing Provider Platform does not yet expose authoritative per-provider lifecycle state.

This is a major boundary decision.

4. Step-05 Pass-B Objective

Pass B will implement a lifecycle eligibility integration boundary, not lifecycle mutation.

It will:

consume CapabilityProviderResolution;
preserve unresolved capability outcomes;
look up the resolved provider using the existing UIProviderManager;
return provider_not_found when the resolved provider identity does not exist;
consume an existing UIProviderLifecycle;
classify lifecycle eligibility without changing lifecycle state.
5. Lifecycle Eligibility Rule

The existing lifecycle vocabulary is:

REGISTERED
INITIALIZED
ACTIVE
INACTIVE

For later runtime consumption, Step-05 will classify:

ACTIVE
    → lifecycle eligible

and:

REGISTERED
INITIALIZED
INACTIVE
    → lifecycle ineligible

Why only ACTIVE?

Because:

REGISTERED

means known but not initialized.

INITIALIZED

means initialized but not necessarily activated.

INACTIVE

explicitly means not active.

ACTIVE

is the only existing state that unambiguously indicates provider activation.

Step-05 will not activate the provider to make it eligible.

That belongs to lifecycle orchestration, not lifecycle observation.

6. Pass-B Result Model

The integration will expose four explicit outcomes:

capability_unresolved
provider_not_found
lifecycle_ineligible
lifecycle_eligible

These are integration outcomes, not new provider lifecycle states.

The distinction is essential:

UIProviderState
    = Provider Platform lifecycle vocabulary

CapabilityProviderLifecycleResult.status
    = Phase-18 integration outcome

No lifecycle vocabulary is duplicated.

/**
 * =============================================================================
 * COREI OPERATING SYSTEM
 *
 * Stage       : 25
 * Phase       : 18
 * Step        : 05
 *
 * File        : capability-provider-lifecycle-result.ts
 * Purpose     : Capability Provider Lifecycle Integration Result
 *
 * -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
 * RESPONSIBILITY
 * -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
 *
 * Defines the explicit outcome of integrating a capability-provider resolution
 * with the existing Provider Platform lifecycle contract.
 *
 * These statuses are integration outcomes.
 *
 * They do not replace, duplicate, or extend UIProviderState.
 *
 * =============================================================================
 */

import type {

    UIProviderLifecycle,
    UIProviderModel

} from "../../providers";

export interface CapabilityProviderLifecycleUnresolved {

    readonly status:
        "capability_unresolved";

    readonly capabilityId: string;

}

export interface CapabilityProviderNotFound {

    readonly status:
        "provider_not_found";

    readonly capabilityId: string;

    readonly providerId: string;

}

export interface CapabilityProviderLifecycleIneligible {

    readonly status:
        "lifecycle_ineligible";

    readonly capabilityId: string;

    readonly providerId: string;

    readonly provider: UIProviderModel;

    readonly lifecycle: UIProviderLifecycle;

}

export interface CapabilityProviderLifecycleEligible {

    readonly status:
        "lifecycle_eligible";

    readonly capabilityId: string;

    readonly providerId: string;

    readonly provider: UIProviderModel;

    readonly lifecycle: UIProviderLifecycle;

}

export type CapabilityProviderLifecycleResult =

    | CapabilityProviderLifecycleUnresolved
    | CapabilityProviderNotFound
    | CapabilityProviderLifecycleIneligible
    | CapabilityProviderLifecycleEligible;
TS

echo
echo "================================================================================"
echo "07 — CREATE LIFECYCLE INTEGRATION"
echo "================================================================================"

cat > "$INTEGRATION_ROOT/capability-provider-lifecycle-integration.ts" <<'TS'
/**
 * =============================================================================
 * COREI OPERATING SYSTEM
 *
 * Stage       : 25
 * Phase       : 18
 * Step        : 05
 *
 * File        : capability-provider-lifecycle-integration.ts
 * Purpose     : Provider Lifecycle Eligibility Integration
 *
 * -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
 * RESPONSIBILITY
 * -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
 *
 * Integrates deterministic capability-provider resolution with the existing
 * UI Provider Platform lifecycle contract.
 *
 * The integration:
 *
 * 1. preserves unresolved capability outcomes,
 * 2. resolves provider identity through UIProviderManager,
 * 3. reports missing provider identities explicitly,
 * 4. observes an existing UIProviderLifecycle,
 * 5. classifies ACTIVE lifecycle state as eligible for later runtime use.
 *
 * -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
 * BOUNDARY
 * -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
 *
 * This integration does not:
 *
 * - create provider lifecycle state,
 * - mutate provider lifecycle state,
 * - initialize providers,
 * - activate providers,
 * - deactivate providers,
 * - dispose providers,
 * - instantiate provider technologies,
 * - execute provider runtime behavior,
 * - perform health-aware failover.
 *
 * =============================================================================
 */

import {

    DefaultUIProviderLifecycle,
    UIProviderManager

} from "../../providers";

import type {

    UIProviderLifecycle

} from "../../providers";

import type {

    CapabilityProviderResolution

} from "./capability-provider-resolution";

import type {

    CapabilityProviderLifecycleResult

} from "./capability-provider-lifecycle-result";

export class CapabilityProviderLifecycleIntegration {

    public constructor(

        private readonly providerManager:
            UIProviderManager,

        private readonly lifecycle:
            UIProviderLifecycle =
                DefaultUIProviderLifecycle

    ) {}

    /**
     * Integrates an existing capability-provider resolution with
     * the existing Provider Platform lifecycle contract.
     */
    public evaluate(
        resolution:
            CapabilityProviderResolution
    ): CapabilityProviderLifecycleResult {

        if (
            resolution.status ===
            "unresolved"
        ) {

            return {

                status:
                    "capability_unresolved",

                capabilityId:
                    resolution.capabilityId

            };

        }

        const provider =
            this.providerManager.findById(
                resolution.providerId
            );

        if (
            !provider
        ) {

            return {

                status:
                    "provider_not_found",

                capabilityId:
                    resolution.capabilityId,

                providerId:
                    resolution.providerId

            };

        }

        if (
            this.lifecycle.state !==
            "ACTIVE"
        ) {

            return {

                status:
                    "lifecycle_ineligible",

                capabilityId:
                    resolution.capabilityId,

                providerId:
                    resolution.providerId,

                provider,

                lifecycle:
                    this.lifecycle

            };

        }

        return {

            status:
                "lifecycle_eligible",

            capabilityId:
                resolution.capabilityId,

            providerId:
                resolution.providerId,

            provider,

            lifecycle:
                this.lifecycle

        };

    }

}
TS

path.write_text(content)
PY

1. Step-06 Objective

consumes the certified CapabilityProviderLifecycleResult;

3. Existing Runtime Contract

From the Step-05 reconciliation, the Provider Platform already exposes a generic runtime state equivalent to:

ProviderRuntime
├── initialized: boolean
└── loaded: boolean

Step-06 must consume this existing runtime state.

It must not invent a second runtime state machine such as:

BOOTING
READY
RUNNING
DEGRADED
FAILED

unless such vocabulary already exists in the Provider Platform.

4. Runtime Eligibility Rule

The deterministic runtime eligibility rule for Step-06 is:

lifecycle status must be lifecycle_eligible
AND
runtime.initialized must be true
AND
runtime.loaded must be true

Therefore:

lifecycle_eligible
+
initialized=true
+
loaded=true

        ↓

runtime_eligible

All other runtime combinations are:

runtime_ineligible

Examples:

Lifecycle	Initialized	Loaded	Result
eligible	false	false	runtime_ineligible
eligible	true	false	runtime_ineligible
eligible	false	true	runtime_ineligible
eligible	true	true	runtime_eligible
ineligible	any	any	upstream result preserved

Step-06 will not mutate either runtime flag.

5. Important Runtime Limitation

As established during lifecycle reconciliation, the current architecture does not expose an authoritative:

providerId → mutable runtime state

registry.

Therefore Step-06 must preserve the same dependency-injection principle used in Step-05:

CapabilityProviderRuntimeIntegration
        │
        └── consumes existing ProviderRuntime observation

It must not falsely claim that each provider currently owns a separately tracked runtime instance.

This is a contract-integration step, not runtime orchestration.

cat > /tmp/corei-phase18-step06-provider-runtime-integration.sh <<'EOF'
#!/usr/bin/env bash

set -euo pipefail

WORKSPACE_ROOT="$HOME/corei/ui/corei-os"
INTEGRATION_ROOT="$WORKSPACE_ROOT/src/capabilities/integration"
EVIDENCE_ROOT="/tmp/corei-phase18-step06"

cd "$WORKSPACE_ROOT"

rm -rf "$EVIDENCE_ROOT"
mkdir -p "$EVIDENCE_ROOT"

echo "================================================================================"
echo "COREI — STAGE-25 PHASE-18 STEP-06"
echo "PROVIDER RUNTIME INTEGRATION"
echo "================================================================================"

echo
echo "MODE=RUNTIME_READINESS_INTEGRATION"

echo
echo "================================================================================"
echo "01 — REPOSITORY IDENTITY"
echo "================================================================================"

echo "WORKSPACE_ROOT=$WORKSPACE_ROOT"
echo "CURRENT_BRANCH=$(git branch --show-current)"
echo "HEAD=$(git rev-parse HEAD)"
echo "TREE=$(git rev-parse HEAD^{tree})"
echo "PHASE-17_FREEZE_TAG_TARGET=$(git rev-list -n 1 stage25-phase17-institutional-shell-v1 2>/dev/null || true)"

echo
echo "================================================================================"
echo "02 — PRE-STEP REPOSITORY STATE"
echo "================================================================================"

git status --short \
    > "$EVIDENCE_ROOT/git-status-before.txt"

cat "$EVIDENCE_ROOT/git-status-before.txt"

echo
echo "================================================================================"
echo "03 — VERIFY CERTIFIED STEP-05 PREREQUISITES"
echo "================================================================================"

for file in \
    src/capabilities/integration/capability-provider-lifecycle-result.ts \
    src/capabilities/integration/capability-provider-lifecycle-integration.ts
do
    test -f "$file"
    echo "PRESENT $file"
done

grep -q \
    '"lifecycle_eligible"' \
    src/capabilities/integration/capability-provider-lifecycle-result.ts

grep -q \
    '"lifecycle_ineligible"' \
    src/capabilities/integration/capability-provider-lifecycle-result.ts

echo "STEP05_PREREQUISITES=PASS"

echo
echo "================================================================================"
echo "04 — DISCOVER AND VERIFY EXISTING PROVIDER RUNTIME CONTRACT"
echo "================================================================================"

RUNTIME_FILES="$EVIDENCE_ROOT/provider-runtime-files.txt"

find src/providers \
    -type f \
    -iname '*runtime*.ts' \
    | sort \
    > "$RUNTIME_FILES"

cat "$RUNTIME_FILES"

GENERIC_RUNTIME_FILE=""

while IFS= read -r file; do

    if grep -q \
        'initialized' \
        "$file" \
        && grep -q \
        'loaded' \
        "$file"
    then

        GENERIC_RUNTIME_FILE="$file"
        break

    fi

done < "$RUNTIME_FILES"

if [ -z "$GENERIC_RUNTIME_FILE" ]; then

    echo "PROVIDER_RUNTIME_CONTRACT_DISCOVERY=FAIL"
    echo "No existing provider runtime contract containing initialized and loaded was found."

    exit 1

fi

echo
echo "GENERIC_RUNTIME_FILE=$GENERIC_RUNTIME_FILE"

sed -n '1,320p' \
    "$GENERIC_RUNTIME_FILE"

grep -q \
    'initialized' \
    "$GENERIC_RUNTIME_FILE"

grep -q \
    'loaded' \
    "$GENERIC_RUNTIME_FILE"

echo "PROVIDER_RUNTIME_CONTRACT_DISCOVERY=PASS"

echo
echo "================================================================================"
echo "05 — VERIFY PROVIDER PUBLIC API EXPOSURE"
echo "================================================================================"

if grep -Rqs \
    'export .*ProviderRuntime' \
    src/providers
then

    echo "PROVIDER_RUNTIME_PUBLIC_API_SIGNAL=PASS"

else

    echo "PROVIDER_RUNTIME_PUBLIC_API_SIGNAL=NOT_DIRECTLY_DETECTED"
    echo "TypeScript validation will verify the authoritative import boundary."

fi

echo
echo "================================================================================"
echo "06 — PRE-IMPLEMENTATION PROTECTED BOUNDARY SNAPSHOT"
echo "================================================================================"

git status --short \
    src/providers \
    src/foundation/capabilities \
    src/foundation/workspace \
    src/shell \
    src/workbench \
    src/app \
    package.json \
    pnpm-lock.yaml \
    > "$EVIDENCE_ROOT/protected-boundary-before.txt" \
    || true

cat "$EVIDENCE_ROOT/protected-boundary-before.txt"

echo
echo "================================================================================"
echo "07 — CREATE RUNTIME INTEGRATION RESULT MODEL"
echo "================================================================================"

cat > "$INTEGRATION_ROOT/capability-provider-runtime-result.ts" <<'TS'
/**
 * =============================================================================
 * COREI OPERATING SYSTEM
 *
 * Stage       : 25
 * Phase       : 18
 * Step        : 06
 *
 * File        : capability-provider-runtime-result.ts
 * Purpose     : Capability Provider Runtime Integration Result
 *
 * -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
 * RESPONSIBILITY
 * -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
 *
 * Defines the explicit outcome of integrating an already evaluated
 * capability-provider lifecycle result with the existing Provider Platform
 * runtime contract.
 *
 * These statuses are integration outcomes.
 *
 * They do not replace:
 *
 * - capability-provider resolution status,
 * - provider lifecycle state,
 * - provider runtime state.
 *
 * =============================================================================
 */

import type {

    ProviderRuntime

} from "../../providers";

import type {

    CapabilityProviderLifecycleIneligible,
    CapabilityProviderLifecycleUnresolved,
    CapabilityProviderNotFound

} from "./capability-provider-lifecycle-result";

export type CapabilityProviderRuntimeUpstreamResult =

    | CapabilityProviderLifecycleUnresolved
    | CapabilityProviderNotFound
    | CapabilityProviderLifecycleIneligible;

export interface CapabilityProviderRuntimeIneligible {

    readonly status:
        "runtime_ineligible";

    readonly capabilityId: string;

    readonly providerId: string;

    readonly runtime: ProviderRuntime;

}

export interface CapabilityProviderRuntimeEligible {

    readonly status:
        "runtime_eligible";

    readonly capabilityId: string;

    readonly providerId: string;

    readonly runtime: ProviderRuntime;

}

export type CapabilityProviderRuntimeResult =

    | CapabilityProviderRuntimeUpstreamResult
    | CapabilityProviderRuntimeIneligible
    | CapabilityProviderRuntimeEligible;
TS

echo
echo "================================================================================"
echo "08 — CREATE RUNTIME INTEGRATION"
echo "================================================================================"

cat > "$INTEGRATION_ROOT/capability-provider-runtime-integration.ts" <<'TS'
/**
 * =============================================================================
 * COREI OPERATING SYSTEM
 *
 * Stage       : 25
 * Phase       : 18
 * Step        : 06
 *
 * File        : capability-provider-runtime-integration.ts
 * Purpose     : Provider Runtime Readiness Integration
 *
 * -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
 * RESPONSIBILITY
 * -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
 *
 * Integrates an existing capability-provider lifecycle result with the
 * existing Provider Platform runtime contract.
 *
 * The integration:
 *
 * 1. preserves upstream non-eligible outcomes,
 * 2. accepts lifecycle-eligible providers for runtime evaluation,
 * 3. observes the existing ProviderRuntime state,
 * 4. classifies initialized + loaded runtime state as eligible.
 *
 * -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
 * BOUNDARY
 * -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
 *
 * This integration does not:
 *
 * - mutate runtime state,
 * - initialize providers,
 * - load providers,
 * - activate providers,
 * - deactivate providers,
 * - dispose providers,
 * - instantiate provider technologies,
 * - execute capability behavior,
 * - perform health-aware failover.
 *
 * =============================================================================
 */

import type {

    ProviderRuntime

} from "../../providers";

import type {

    CapabilityProviderLifecycleResult

} from "./capability-provider-lifecycle-result";

import type {

    CapabilityProviderRuntimeResult

} from "./capability-provider-runtime-result";

export class CapabilityProviderRuntimeIntegration {

    public constructor(

        private readonly runtime:
            ProviderRuntime

    ) {}

    /**
     * Integrates lifecycle eligibility with existing provider
     * runtime readiness.
     */
    public evaluate(
        lifecycleResult:
            CapabilityProviderLifecycleResult
    ): CapabilityProviderRuntimeResult {

        if (
            lifecycleResult.status !==
            "lifecycle_eligible"
        ) {

            return lifecycleResult;

        }

        if (
            !this.runtime.initialized ||
            !this.runtime.loaded
        ) {

            return {

                status:
                    "runtime_ineligible",

                capabilityId:
                    lifecycleResult.capabilityId,

                providerId:
                    lifecycleResult.providerId,

                runtime:
                    this.runtime

            };

        }

        return {

            status:
                "runtime_eligible",

            capabilityId:
                lifecycleResult.capabilityId,

            providerId:
                lifecycleResult.providerId,

            runtime:
                this.runtime

        };

    }

}
TS

required_exports = [

]

if not content.endswith("\n"):
    content += "\n"

for export_line in required_exports:
    if export_line not in content:
        content += f"{export_line}\n"

path.write_text(content)

1. Step-07 Objective

Step-07 must determine whether the existing Provider Platform already owns authoritative contracts for:

FAILURE
HEALTH
AVAILABILITY

and then integrate against those contracts without inventing parallel infrastructure.

The intended conceptual chain is:

CAPABILITY
    ↓
BINDING
    ↓
RESOLUTION
    ↓
PROVIDER LOOKUP
    ↓
LIFECYCLE ELIGIBILITY
    ↓
RUNTIME READINESS
    ↓
FAILURE / HEALTH / AVAILABILITY
    ↓
CONSUMPTION ELIGIBILITY

However, Step-06 established an important architectural constraint:

The existing Provider Platform does not expose authoritative per-provider runtime-state storage through the current integration boundary.

Therefore we must not assume that it already has:

providerId → health state
providerId → availability state
providerId → failure state

Before implementation, those facts must be reconciled from the repository.

Step-07 will use the same controlled process as Step-05:

READ-ONLY FAILURE / HEALTH / AVAILABILITY RECONCILIATION

        ↓

ARCHITECTURAL DECISION

        ↓

PASS B
PRECISE INTEGRATION IMPLEMENTATION

We will not write Step-07 integration code before determining:

whether health contracts already exist;
whether availability contracts already exist;
whether failure contracts already exist;
where those contracts are owned;
whether they are generic or UI-provider-specific;
whether they are per-provider or global;
whether they are state, telemetry, diagnostics, or operational status;
whether Phase-18 is authorized to consume them.

This avoids creating a second:

ProviderHealth
ProviderAvailability
ProviderFailure

model that conflicts with existing architecture.

2. Critical Architectural Finding

The repository does not have one unified failure/health/availability model.

Instead, it contains several contracts at different architectural layers.

Existing contracts
A. Foundation health
src/foundation/health/

Authoritative vocabulary:

UNKNOWN
HEALTHY
DEGRADED
UNHEALTHY

This is the generic platform foundation health framework.

It must not be modified or duplicated.

B. Provider runtime readiness
src/providers/runtime/provider-runtime.ts

Existing state:

initialized: boolean
loaded: boolean

Step-06 already consumes this contract and produces:

runtime_ineligible
runtime_eligible

This represents runtime readiness, not health.

C. Service health and availability
src/services/state/

Existing vocabulary:

ServiceHealth
    HEALTHY
    DEGRADED
    UNHEALTHY

ServiceAvailability
    AVAILABLE
    UNAVAILABLE

ServiceReadiness
    READY
    NOT_READY

These contracts belong to the Service Platform.

They must not become provider contracts.

D. Platform runtime health
src/platform-runtime/state/

Existing vocabulary:

UNKNOWN
HEALTHY
DEGRADED
WARNING
FAILED
RECOVERING

This belongs to Platform Runtime.

It must not be imported as the provider health authority.

E. Shell operational status
src/status/

Existing vocabulary:

connected
available
inactive

The source itself explicitly states that this is:

Shell integration presentation state only.

It is not live health telemetry and must remain completely outside Step-07 provider eligibility.

3. The Most Important Pass-A Finding

The reconciliation reports:

PROVIDER_SPECIFIC_SIGNAL_COUNT=0

Therefore, there is currently no authoritative Provider Platform contract for:

provider failure,
provider health,
provider availability.

This means Step-07 cannot simply wire an existing provider-specific contract because one does not exist.

However, Step-07 also must not modify the certified Provider Platform merely to introduce speculative provider health infrastructure.

The correct architecture is therefore:

Create a Phase-18 integration-owned observation boundary that consumes provider runtime eligibility and externally supplied failure, health, and availability observations.

This preserves ownership.

4. Correct Pass-B Architecture

The pipeline should become:

Capability
    │
    ▼
Binding Registry
    │
    ▼
Provider Resolution
    │
    ▼
Lifecycle Eligibility
    │
    ▼
Runtime Eligibility
    │
    ▼
Failure / Health / Availability Integration
    │
    ▼
Operational Eligibility

The Step-07 boundary should be:

CapabilityProviderRuntimeResult
        +
ProviderOperationalObservation
        │
        ▼
CapabilityProviderOperationalIntegration
        │
        ▼
CapabilityProviderOperationalResult

This is intentionally observation-based, not ownership-based.

5. Pass-B Must Not Do

Step-07 must not:

modify src/providers/**,
modify src/foundation/health/**,
modify src/services/**,
modify src/platform-runtime/**,
modify src/status/**,
create a new global health framework,
duplicate HealthStatus,
reuse ServiceAvailability as provider ownership,
use Shell OperationalStatusState,
mutate provider runtime,
perform failover,
select another provider,
activate or deactivate providers,
instantiate technology adapters,
execute capability behavior,
catch arbitrary runtime exceptions,
convert Step-07 into observability infrastructure.

This keeps Step-07 strictly within:

src/capabilities/integration/
6. Recommended Step-07 Contracts
File 1
src/capabilities/integration/capability-provider-operational-observation.ts

Recommended contract:

export type CapabilityProviderHealthStatus =
    | "UNKNOWN"
    | "HEALTHY"
    | "DEGRADED"
    | "UNHEALTHY";

export interface CapabilityProviderOperationalObservation {

    readonly failed: boolean;

    readonly health:
        CapabilityProviderHealthStatus;

    readonly available: boolean;

}
Why this shape?

Because the three dimensions are semantically independent:

failure      = explicit failure condition
health       = quality/degradation condition
availability = whether provider can currently participate

For example:

Failed	Health	Available	Meaning
false	HEALTHY	true	eligible
false	DEGRADED	true	degraded but usable
false	UNHEALTHY	true	blocked
false	HEALTHY	false	unavailable
true	any	any	failed
7. Important Refinement: Do Not Duplicate the Health Vocabulary

There is one architectural issue with defining:

CapabilityProviderHealthStatus

as another independent string union.

The Foundation already owns:

HealthStatus

Therefore the stronger institutional implementation is:

import type {
    HealthStatus
} from "../../foundation";

Then:

export interface CapabilityProviderOperationalObservation {

    readonly failed: boolean;

    readonly health: HealthStatus;

    readonly available: boolean;

}
Recommended decision

Reuse HealthStatus from Foundation.

Why:

Foundation health is explicitly described as the authoritative platform health contract.
Its vocabulary already provides exactly the required four states.
Step-07 consumes health semantics; it does not own them.
This avoids a second health vocabulary.

Therefore:

Health vocabulary ownership
        =
Foundation

Provider operational observation ownership
        =
Phase-18 Capability ↔ Provider Integration

This is the cleanest boundary.

8. Result Contract
File 2
src/capabilities/integration/capability-provider-operational-result.ts

Recommended statuses:

provider_failed
provider_health_unknown
provider_unhealthy
provider_unavailable
operational_eligible

Plus all upstream Step-06 outcomes.

Recommended result hierarchy:

export type CapabilityProviderOperationalUpstreamResult =
    Exclude<
        CapabilityProviderRuntimeResult,
        CapabilityProviderRuntimeEligible
    >;

However, I recommend not using a complex utility-type dependency here.

Use explicit imports, consistent with Step-06:

export type CapabilityProviderOperationalUpstreamResult =

    | CapabilityProviderLifecycleUnresolved
    | CapabilityProviderNotFound
    | CapabilityProviderLifecycleIneligible
    | CapabilityProviderRuntimeIneligible;

Then define:

provider_failed
provider_health_unknown
provider_unhealthy
provider_unavailable
operational_eligible
9. Deterministic Evaluation Order

This is critical.

Pass-B must use one explicit precedence order:

1. Preserve upstream non-runtime-eligible result
2. Check explicit failure
3. Check UNKNOWN health
4. Check UNHEALTHY health
5. Check availability
6. Return operationally eligible

Recommended implementation:

runtime result not runtime_eligible
    → preserve upstream result

failed === true
    → provider_failed

health === UNKNOWN
    → provider_health_unknown

health === UNHEALTHY
    → provider_unhealthy

available === false
    → provider_unavailable

otherwise
    → operational_eligible
10. DEGRADED Health Decision

A provider with:

health = DEGRADED
available = true
failed = false

should remain:

operational_eligible

The result should preserve the observation:

{
    status: "operational_eligible",
    ...
    observation: {
        failed: false,
        health: HealthStatus.DEGRADED,
        available: true
    }
}
Why?

Because:

DEGRADED ≠ UNHEALTHY

Blocking all degraded providers would silently redefine Foundation health semantics.

Later stages can introduce:

policy,
risk thresholds,
failover preference,
degraded-provider routing.

Step-07 should only determine minimum operational eligibility.

11. UNKNOWN Health Decision

I recommend:

UNKNOWN → provider_health_unknown

and therefore not eligible.

This follows COREI's deterministic safety posture:

Unknown operational state must not be silently interpreted as healthy.

For a professional trading platform, especially where providers may later support:

execution,
market data,
charting,
terminal operations,
workflow editing,

the safe rule is:

UNKNOWN ≠ HEALTHY
12. Failure Precedence

Failure should have the highest Step-07 precedence.

Example:

failed = true
health = UNHEALTHY
available = false

Result:

provider_failed

Not:

provider_unhealthy

and not:

provider_unavailable

Deterministic precedence:

FAILURE
    >
HEALTH UNKNOWN / UNHEALTHY
    >
AVAILABILITY
    >
ELIGIBLE

This ensures the same observation always produces exactly one canonical outcome.

13. Proposed Pass-B File Scope

Exactly three files:

src/capabilities/integration/
├── capability-provider-operational-observation.ts
├── capability-provider-operational-result.ts
├── capability-provider-operational-integration.ts
└── index.ts                              # update only
No other source files should change.

This is the appropriate institutional scope.

14. Exact Step-07 Responsibility
CapabilityProviderOperationalIntegration

Input:

CapabilityProviderRuntimeResult
+
CapabilityProviderOperationalObservation

Output:

CapabilityProviderOperationalResult

Its responsibility:

preserve all upstream outcomes,
accept only runtime_eligible for operational evaluation,
observe failure,
observe health,
observe availability,
classify final operational eligibility.

It does not:

resolve
initialize
load
activate
deactivate
dispose
fail over
retry
recover
execute
15. Final Status Chain After Step-07

The complete deterministic outcome vocabulary becomes:

capability_unresolved
        │
        ▼
provider_not_found
        │
        ▼
lifecycle_ineligible
        │
        ▼
runtime_ineligible
        │
        ▼
provider_failed
        │
        ▼
provider_health_unknown
        │
        ▼
provider_unhealthy
        │
        ▼
provider_unavailable
        │
        ▼
operational_eligible

This gives Phase-18 a clean progressive eligibility pipeline without changing ownership of any underlying platform contract.

16. Pass-B Certification Requirements

The implementation should validate at least these cases:

Test	Expected
capability_unresolved	preserved unchanged
provider_not_found	preserved unchanged
lifecycle_ineligible	preserved unchanged
runtime_ineligible	preserved unchanged
failed provider	provider_failed
unknown health	provider_health_unknown
unhealthy provider	provider_unhealthy
healthy but unavailable	provider_unavailable
degraded + available	operational_eligible
healthy + available	operational_eligible

And repository validation:

pnpm exec tsc --noEmit
pnpm build

Boundary validation:

Only:
src/capabilities/integration/

should change.

Final Decision

Proceed with Pass-B.

The Pass-A evidence supports the following authoritative Step-07 implementation:

Stage-25 Phase-18 Step-07 shall add an integration-owned, read-only operational eligibility boundary that consumes the existing Step-06 runtime result plus externally supplied failure, Foundation health, and availability observations. It shall preserve all upstream outcomes and deterministically classify provider failure, unknown/unhealthy health, unavailability, or operational eligibility.

The implementation must remain entirely under:

src/capabilities/integration/

and must not alter the certified Provider Platform or any existing Foundation, Service, Platform Runtime, or Shell status contract.

Recommended next action: implement Step-07 Pass-B with exactly 3 new files + 1 public API update, followed by deterministic contract tests and full TypeScript/build/boundary validation.

Stage-25 → Phase-18 → Step-07 — Pass-B Implementation

Capability Resolution
    ↓

Provider Runtime Eligibility

Failure / Health / Availability Evaluation
    ↓
Operational Eligibility
Change scope
CREATE  src/capabilities/integration/capability-provider-operational-observation.ts

No existing Provider Platform, Foundation Health, Service, Platform Runtime, or Shell status contract will be modified.
